## P4 - `configCheckErrors` emits a stray two-space line when `vtysh` fails with no output

**Location:** [nodes/frr/frr.go:324-326](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/nodes/frr/frr.go#L324-L326)

**Problem:** When `vtysh --dryrun` exits nonzero with completely empty stdout and stderr, the fallback branch in `configCheckErrors` returns `"  "` &#40;the two-space indent with nothing after it&#41;. The resulting error renders as the &#34;has errors that FRR skips at startup&#34; header, an empty indented line, and then the fix hint, with no cause at all between them.

**Trigger:** A nonzero exit with no output, for example a `vtysh` crash or the exec being killed &#40;e.g., exit 137&#41;, on any `frr` node during `deploy`.

**Impact:** Cosmetic confusion; the user gets an error message with an empty line and no explanation of what failed.

**Suggested correction:** In the `out == ""` case return an empty string &#40;or a short &#34;`vtysh` exited with code %d without output&#34; line&#41; so the message stays clean.


## P4 - `configFixHint` default-case wording can point users at the lab copy, which `--reconfigure` overwrites

**Location:** [nodes/frr/frr.go:342-345](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/nodes/frr/frr.go#L342-L345)

**Problem:** The default hint reads &#34;It is the lab&#39;s copy of `<startup-config>`, which later deploys keep&#58; fix that file and `deploy` again with `--reconfigure`.&#34; The most natural referent of &#34;that file&#34; is the lab&#39;s copy emphasized in the previous clause. But `--reconfigure` deletes the entire lab directory &#40;`os.RemoveAll(TopologyLabDir())` in [`core/deploy.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/core/deploy.go#L1-L1)&#41; and regenerates `frr.conf` from the `startup-config` source, so a user who follows the literal reading loses their edit and hits the same error. The intended workflow &#40;fix the `startup-config`, then `--reconfigure`&#41; is stated correctly in the new Configuration check docs section, but the error message stands alone in `deploy` output.

**Trigger:** Any `frr` node with a `startup-config` whose rendered config fails `vtysh --dryrun`; the user follows the hint as literally written.

**Impact:** A wasted editing round and a puzzling repeat of the same failure; no data loss beyond an edit to a generated file.

**Suggested correction:** Name the `startup-config` explicitly in the hint, e.g. &#34;Fix `<startup-config>` and `deploy` again with `--reconfigure`, which regenerates the lab copy from it.&#34; The enforce and no-startup-config variants are unambiguous and need no change.


## Coverage

- All three changed paths were reviewed against the base revision; the diff is entirely the new FRR config-check feature &#40;`checkConfig`, `configCheckErrors`, `configFixHint`, tests, docs section&#41;.
- Verified the error aggregation chain matches the doc example&#58; [`core/deploy.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/core/deploy.go#L1-L1) `waitForNodeDeploy` &#40;&#34;deployment failed for one or more nodes&#58; %w&#34;&#41; plus `postDeployApplyNodes`/[`core/clab.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/core/clab.go#L1-L1) &#40;&#34;node %q post-deploy&#58; %w&#34;&#41;.
- Verified `RunExec` &#40;[`nodes/default_node.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/nodes/default_node.go#L1-L1)&#41; returns nil on nonzero exit codes and reports them via `ExecResult.GetReturnCode()`, so `checkConfig`&#39;s return-code handling is correct.
- Verified the config-save wrapper passes non-`-w`/`--writeconfig` invocations &#40;including `--dryrun`&#41; to the real `vtysh`, so the check is not affected by the wrapper.
- Verified the fix workflows against `GenerateConfig`, `--reconfigure`, and `redeploy` semantics; the doc&#39;s claims about when the lab copy is kept or regenerated are accurate.
- Unit tests cover the hint variants, rc handling, line filtering, empty-output fallback, and `PostDeploy` ordering; the in-repo Robot suite &#40;[`tests/14-basic-frr/01-frr.robot`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/tests/14-basic-frr/01-frr.robot#L1-L1)&#41; deploys valid configs but has no e2e case for a failing config.
- The real `vtysh --dryrun` exit-code and `line N:` output-format behavior cannot be verified from the snapshot &#40;mocks only&#41;; it should be confirmed against the pinned FRR image in a real CI run. Older plain-FRR images would at worst make the check a no-op &#40;exit 0&#41;, not a false positive.
- No topology schema keys were added or changed &#40;`startup-config`, `enforce-startup-config`, `suppress-startup-config`, `daemons` all pre-exist&#41;, so no [`clab.schema.json`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/d98a60b529eda6603c5fa535c972a75d71e9cc8e/schemas/clab.schema.json#L1-L1) work is required.
- No `docs/rn/` changes in the diff, so the release-note review rule does not apply. No CI formatting failures were supplied; the code appears gofmt-consistent.
- Source-only review&#58; no tests or container runs were executed; findings are from static analysis.

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

If uncertain, ask.\n19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n22: \n23: ## 2. Simplicity First\n24: \n25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n26: \n27: - No features beyond what was asked.\n28: - No abstractions for single-use code.\n29: - No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n32: \n33: Ask yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n34: \n35: ## 3. Surgical Changes\n36: \n37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n38: \n39: When editing existing code:\n40: \n41: - Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n45: \n46: When your changes create orphans:\n47: \n48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n50: \n51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n52: \n53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n54: \n55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n56: \n57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:\n58: \n59: - \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n60: - \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n61: - \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n62: \n63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n64: \n65: ```\n66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n69: ```\n70: \n71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n72: \n73: ---\n74: \n75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.\n\n(End of file - total 75 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Karpathy behavioral guidelines\n\nBehavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.\n\n**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.\n\n## 1. Think Before Coding\n\n**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n\nBefore implementing:\n\n- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n\n## 2. Simplicity First\n\n**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

6:  
7:  Without a `startup-config` the node comes up with a minimal configuration that only sets `frr defaults traditional` and logging.
8:  
9:  The hostname is not set in the generated config on purpose. FRR picks up the container's hostname, which containerlab already sets to the node name.
10:  
11: +### Configuration check
12: +
13: +FRR skips any line of `frr.conf` it cannot parse and starts with the rest of the file, so a typo would leave a node running with part of its configuration missing and the mistake visible only in its log. To catch this, the deploy checks each node's `frr.conf` with `vtysh --dryrun` and fails if the file does not parse, quoting each line FRR would skip:
14: +
15: +```
16: +deployment failed for one or more nodes: node "router1" post-deploy:
17: +clab-frr01/router1/config/frr.conf has errors that FRR skips at startup, leaving those lines unconfigured:
18: +  line 5: % Unknown command[21]:  ip adress 10.0.0.1/32
19: +It is the lab's copy of router1/frr.conf, which later deploys keep: fix that file and deploy again with --reconfigure.
20: +```
21: +
22: +The node that failed the check is left running, with its ssh keys and password in place, so it can be inspected.
23: +
24: +The file checked is the lab directory's copy, which is the one the node uses. Created from `startup-config` on the first deploy, it is kept by later deploys unless [`enforce-startup-config`](../nodes.md#enforce-startup-config) is set, which is why a fixed `startup-config` needs `--reconfigure` to reach the node.
25: +
26: +The check covers what does not parse: unknown commands, invalid values and incomplete commands. A line that parses but that a daemon then rejects, such as a BGP neighbor assigned to a peer-group that does not exist, still deploys, as does configuration for a daemon that is not enabled in [`daemons`](#daemons). Look for `processing failure` in `docker logs <node>` to find the first kind.
27: +
28:  ### Daemons
29:  
30:  By default all daemons supported by this kind are enabled. To run only the daemons a lab actually needs, list them under the `daemons` [kind-specific config key](../nodes.md#kind-specific-config):

82: +// parse. FRR skips such a line, applies the rest of the file and keeps
83: +// running, and says so only in its log, so a typo would otherwise deploy
84: +// successfully with part of the configuration missing. vtysh's dry-run mode
85: +// parses the file without applying it, so it needs no running daemons.
86: +//

90: +func (n *frr) checkConfig(ctx context.Context) error {
91: +	confPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)
92: +
93: +	// suppress-startup-config leaves no frr.conf to check.
94: +	if !clabutils.FileExists(confPath) {
95: +		return nil
96: +	}
97: +
98: +	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(
99: +		[]string{"vtysh", "--dryrun", "--inputfile", filepath.Join(etcFRR, frrConfFile)})
100: +
101: +	result, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
102: +	if err != nil {
103: +		return fmt.Errorf("failed to check the FRR configuration: %w", err)
104: +	}
105: +
106: +	if result.GetReturnCode() == 0 {
107: +		return nil
108: +	}
109: +
110: +	return fmt.Errorf(
111: +		"%s has errors that FRR skips at startup, leaving those lines unconfigured:\n%s\n%s",
112: +		confPath, configCheckErrors(result), n.configFixHint())
113: +}
114: +
115: +// configCheckErrors returns the lines of a failed dry run that name a line of
116: +// frr.conf, or all of its output if none does.
117: +func configCheckErrors(result *clabexec.ExecResult) string {
118: +	out := strings.TrimSpace(result.GetStdOutString() + "\n" + result.GetStdErrString())
119: +
120: +	var errs []string
121: +
122: +	for l := range strings.SplitSeq(out, "\n") {
123: +		if configErrorLine.MatchString(l) {
124: +			errs = append(errs, "  "+strings.TrimSpace(l))
125: +		}
126: +	}
127: +
128: +	if len(errs) == 0 {
129: +		return "  " + strings.ReplaceAll(out, "\n", "\n  ")
130: +	}
131: +
132: +	return strings.Join(errs, "\n")
133: +}
134: +
135: +// configFixHint says where to fix the configuration. Where it came from a
136: +// startup-config, the lab directory holds a copy that later deploys keep, so
137: +// fixing the startup-config alone would not reach the node.
138: +func (n *frr) configFixHint() string {
139: +	switch {
140: +	case n.Cfg.StartupConfig == "":
141: +		return "Fix it, then destroy the lab without --cleanup and deploy again, " +
142: +			"which keeps the file."
143: +	case n.Cfg.EnforceStartupConfig:
144: +		return fmt.Sprintf("It is copied from %s on every deploy: fix that file and deploy again.",

146: +	default:
147: +		return fmt.Sprintf("It is the lab's copy of %s, which later deploys keep: "+
148: +			"fix that file and deploy again with --reconfigure.", n.Cfg.StartupConfig)
149: +	}
150:  }

26: var kindNames = []string{"frr", "frrouting"}

28: const (
29: 	generateable     = true
30: 	generateIfFormat = "eth%d"
31: 
32: 	// cfgDir holds the three files bind mounted over the container's /etc/frr.
33: 	cfgDir = "config"
34: 
35: 	frrConfFile   = "frr.conf"
36: 	daemonsFile   = "daemons"
37: 	vtyshConfFile = "vtysh.conf"
38: 
39: 	etcFRR = "/etc/frr"
40: 
41: 	// authzKeysTargets are the "user:home" pairs whose authorized_keys file is
42: 	// populated: root for a shell and admin for vtysh, both reached with the
43: 	// same key.
44: 	authzKeysTargets = "root:/root admin:/home/admin"
45: 
46: 	// authzKeysGuard skips a user the image does not have. admin ships only in
47: 	// the containerlab flavour, so a plain release image still gets root's keys
48: 	// instead of an error.
49: 	authzKeysGuard = `id -u "$user" >/dev/null 2>&1 || continue`
50: 
51: 	// authzKeysChown hands the file to the user who logs in with it; sshd
52: 	// rejects an authorized_keys that user does not own.
53: 	authzKeysChown = `chown -R "$user:" "$home/.ssh"`
54: 
55: 	// authzKeysScript writes the public keys, passed to it as $1, to the
56: 	// authorized_keys of every user in authzKeysTargets that exists.
57: 	authzKeysScript = `set -e
58: for entry in ` + authzKeysTargets + `; do
59: 	user=${entry%%:*}
60: 	home=${entry#*:}
61: 
62: 	` + authzKeysGuard + `
63: 
64: 	mkdir -p "$home/.ssh"
65: 	chmod 700 "$home/.ssh"
66: 	# The file is removed rather than truncated: a redirect onto an existing
67: 	# file keeps that file's ownership, and sshd rejects an authorized_keys
68: 	# that its user does not own.
69: 	rm -f "$home/.ssh/authorized_keys"
70: 	printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$home/.ssh/authorized_keys"
71: 	chmod 600 "$home/.ssh/authorized_keys"
72: 	` + authzKeysChown + `
73: done`
74: 
75: 	// passwordGuard skips a user the image does not have. A plain release image
76: 	// has no admin, and root there is still reachable with the host's keys.
77: 	passwordGuard = `id -u "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0`
78: 
79: 	// passwordScript sets the password of the user named by $1 to $2. Both
80: 	// travel as arguments rather than in the script text, so no character in
81: 	// the password can be read as shell.
82: 	passwordScript = passwordGuard + `
83: printf '%s:%s\n' "$1" "$2" | chpasswd`
84: 
85: 	// watchfrr invokes vtysh -w for CLI saves. Repair the bind-mounted file
86: 	// before returning, while ordinary vtysh sessions still exec the binary.
87: 	configSaveWrapper = `#!/bin/sh
88: case "$*" in
89:     -w|--writeconfig)
90:         "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" "$@" || exit $?
91:         chown %d:%d "/etc/frr/frr.conf" || exit $?
92:         chmod %04o "/etc/frr/frr.conf"
93:         ;;
94:     *)
95:         exec "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" "$@"
96:         ;;
97: esac`
98: 
99: 	// no-header keeps the "Building configuration..." preamble out of the
100: 	// saved file, which is written back as the node's frr.conf.
101: 	saveCmd = `vtysh -c "show running-config no-header"`
102: )
103: 
104: var (
105: 	// configErrorLine matches the lines in which vtysh names a line of the
106: 	// configuration it could not parse, e.g. "line 5: % Unknown command[21]: ...".
107: 	configErrorLine = regexp.MustCompile(`^\s*line \d+:`)
108: 
109: 	// defaultCredentials is the admin user the containerlab image ships, whose
110: 	// login shell is vtysh. It is what "ssh <node>" uses, so a bare ssh to a
111: 	// node reaches the routing CLI; "ssh root@<node>" still gets a shell. The
112: 	// image has no password for admin: PostDeploy sets this one.
113: 	defaultCredentials = clabnodes.NewCredentials("admin", "admin")
114: 
115: 	//go:embed frr.cfg
116: 	defaultCfgTemplate string
117: 
118: 	// vtyshCfg turns on integrated config so that frr.conf is the single
119: 	// configuration file, matching what FRR ships upstream.
120: 	//go:embed vtysh.conf
121: 	vtyshCfg string
122: )
123: 
124: // KindSpecificConfig is the frr kind-specific config, set as keys on the node definition.
125: type KindSpecificConfig struct {
126: 	// Daemons is the list of FRR routing daemons to enable. When empty, all
127: 	// daemons known to the kind are enabled. The always-on daemons (zebra,
128: 	// staticd, mgmtd, watchfrr) need not be listed.
129: 	Daemons []string `yaml:"daemons,omitempty" json:"daemons,omitempty"`
130: }

132: var kindSpecificConfig clabnodes.KindSpecificConfigSpec[KindSpecificConfig]
133: 
134: func (n *frr) kindSpecificCfg() *KindSpecificConfig { return kindSpecificConfig.Of(n.Cfg) }

136: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
137: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
138: 	generateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)
139: 
140: 	// FRR has no scrapli or napalm platform, so no PlatformAttrs are set.
141: 	nrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(
142: 		defaultCredentials, generateNodeAttributes, nil,
143: 	).WithKindSpecificConfig(kindSpecificConfig)
144: 
145: 	r.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {
146: 		return new(frr)
147: 	}, nrea)
148: }

150: type frr struct {
151: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
152: 
153: 	sshPubKeys []ssh.PublicKey
154: }
155: 
156: func (n *frr) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
157: 	// Init DefaultNode
158: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
159: 
160: 	n.Cfg = cfg
161: 	for _, o := range opts {
162: 		o(n)
163: 	}
164: 
165: 	// The whole directory is mounted rather than the three files individually.
166: 	// FRR saves a configuration by renaming frr.conf to frr.conf.sav and
167: 	// writing a new one, and a single-file bind mount cannot be renamed, so
168: 	// per-file mounts make every "write memory" report
169: 	//
170: 	//	Error renaming /etc/frr/frr.conf to /etc/frr/frr.conf.sav: Resource busy
171: 	//
172: 	// and lose the backup. Mounting the directory costs nothing: the image
173: 	// ships only daemons in /etc/frr, and containerlab writes that file too.
174: 	n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds,
175: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir), ":", etcFRR),
176: 	)
177: 
178: 	// FRR programs routes into the kernel, which only forwards if asked to.
179: 	n.Cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv4.ip_forward"] = "1"
180: 	n.Cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv6.conf.all.forwarding"] = "1"
181: 
182: 	return nil
183: }
184: 
185: func (n *frr) PreDeploy(_ context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {
186: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
187: 
188: 	_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)
189: 	if err != nil {
190: 		return err
191: 	}
192: 
193: 	// Recorded here and written to the container in PostDeploy, once it runs.
194: 	n.sshPubKeys = params.SSHPubKeys
195: 
196: 	return n.createFRRFiles()
197: }
198: 
199: func (n *frr) createFRRFiles() error {
200: 	nodeCfg := n.Config()
201: 
202: 	dir := filepath.Join(nodeCfg.LabDir, cfgDir)
203: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(dir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
204: 
205: 	// frr.conf comes from the user's startup-config when given, and from the
206: 	// bundled template otherwise. Routing it through GenerateConfig is what
207: 	// makes enforce-startup-config, suppress-startup-config and templating
208: 	// behave the same way here as for every other kind.
209: 	cfgTemplate := defaultCfgTemplate
210: 
211: 	if nodeCfg.StartupConfig != "" {
212: 		c, err := os.ReadFile(nodeCfg.StartupConfig)
213: 		if err != nil {
214: 			return err
215: 		}
216: 
217: 		cfgTemplate = string(c)
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	confPath := filepath.Join(dir, frrConfFile)
221: 
222: 	err := n.GenerateConfig(confPath, cfgTemplate)
223: 	if err != nil {
224: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s, failed to generate config: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
225: 	}
226: 
227: 	// GenerateConfig keeps an existing frr.conf, which may be one a previous
228: 	// "write memory" left owned by frr and unreadable, so repair it here too
229: 	// rather than only on save.
230: 	err = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)
231: 	if err != nil {
232: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
233: 	}
234: 
235: 	rendered, err := renderDaemons(n.kindSpecificCfg().Daemons)
236: 	if err != nil {
237: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
238: 	}
239: 
240: 	err = clabutils.CreateFile(filepath.Join(dir, daemonsFile), rendered)
241: 	if err != nil {
242: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s, failed to write daemons file: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
243: 	}
244: 
245: 	err = clabutils.CreateFile(filepath.Join(dir, vtyshConfFile), vtyshCfg)
246: 	if err != nil {
247: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s, failed to write vtysh.conf: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	return nil
251: }
252: 
253: // PostDeploy installs login credentials and keeps CLI-saved configs readable.
254: func (n *frr) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
255: 	log.Debugf("Running postdeploy actions for frr %q node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
256: 
257: 	err := n.addSSHKeys(ctx)
258: 	if err != nil {
259: 		return err
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	err = n.setPassword(ctx)
263: 	if err != nil {
264: 		return err
265: 	}
266: 
267: 	err = n.installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx)
268: 	if err != nil {
269: 		return err
270: 	}
271: 
272: 	// Last, so that a node with a broken configuration can still be logged
273: 	// into to look at it.
274: 	return n.checkConfig(ctx)
275: }
276: 
277: // checkConfig fails the deploy when the node's frr.conf has lines FRR cannot
278: // parse. FRR skips such a line, applies the rest of the file and keeps
279: // running, and says so only in its log, so a typo would otherwise deploy
280: // successfully with part of the configuration missing. vtysh's dry-run mode
281: // parses the file without applying it, so it needs no running daemons.
282: //
283: // It catches what vtysh itself rejects: unknown commands, bad values and
284: // incomplete commands. A line that parses but that a daemon refuses, or
285: // configuration for a daemon that is not enabled, still goes unnoticed.
286: func (n *frr) checkConfig(ctx context.Context) error {
287: 	confPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)
288: 
289: 	// suppress-startup-config leaves no frr.conf to check.
290: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(confPath) {
291: 		return nil
292: 	}
293: 
294: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(
295: 		[]string{"vtysh", "--dryrun", "--inputfile", filepath.Join(etcFRR, frrConfFile)})
296: 
297: 	result, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
298: 	if err != nil {
299: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to check the FRR configuration: %w", err)
300: 	}
301: 
302: 	if result.GetReturnCode() == 0 {
303: 		return nil
304: 	}
305: 
306: 	return fmt.Errorf(
307: 		"%s has errors that FRR skips at startup, leaving those lines unconfigured:\n%s\n%s",
308: 		confPath, configCheckErrors(result), n.configFixHint())
309: }
310: 
311: // configCheckErrors returns the lines of a failed dry run that name a line of
312: // frr.conf, or all of its output if none does.
313: func configCheckErrors(result *clabexec.ExecResult) string {
314: 	out := strings.TrimSpace(result.GetStdOutString() + "\n" + result.GetStdErrString())
315: 
316: 	var errs []string
317: 
318: 	for l := range strings.SplitSeq(out, "\n") {
319: 		if configErrorLine.MatchString(l) {
320: 			errs = append(errs, "  "+strings.TrimSpace(l))
321: 		}
322: 	}
323: 
324: 	if len(errs) == 0 {
325: 		return "  " + strings.ReplaceAll(out, "\n", "\n  ")
326: 	}
327: 
328: 	return strings.Join(errs, "\n")
329: }
330: 
331: // configFixHint says where to fix the configuration. Where it came from a
332: // startup-config, the lab directory holds a copy that later deploys keep, so
333: // fixing the startup-config alone would not reach the node.
334: func (n *frr) configFixHint() string {
335: 	switch {
336: 	case n.Cfg.StartupConfig == "":
337: 		return "Fix it, then destroy the lab without --cleanup and deploy again, " +
338: 			"which keeps the file."
339: 	case n.Cfg.EnforceStartupConfig:
340: 		return fmt.Sprintf("It is copied from %s on every deploy: fix that file and deploy again.",
341: 			n.Cfg.StartupConfig)
342: 	default:
343: 		return fmt.Sprintf("It is the lab's copy of %s, which later deploys keep: "+
344: 			"fix that file and deploy again with --reconfigure.", n.Cfg.StartupConfig)
345: 	}
346: }
347: 
348: func (n *frr) installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx context.Context) error {
349: 	uid, gid, err := clabutils.GetRealUserIDs()
350: 	if err != nil {
351: 		return err
352: 	}
353: 
354: 	// FRR can execute vtysh during PostDeploy. A same-directory hard link keeps
355: 	// the original available and preserves its metadata, including symlink identity.
356: 	// Publish the wrapper atomically only after the backup exists.
357: 	// Preserve the original backup across sequential PostDeploy calls.
358: 	script := `set -e
359: wrapper_tmp=''
360: trap 'rm -f -- "$wrapper_tmp"' EXIT
361: if [ ! -f "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" ]; then
362:     ln -T -- "/usr/bin/vtysh" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"
363: fi
364: wrapper_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX")
365: printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$wrapper_tmp"
366: chmod 755 "$wrapper_tmp"
367: mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"`
368: 
369: 	wrapper := fmt.Sprintf(configSaveWrapper, uid, gid, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)
370: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice([]string{"bash", "-c", script, "--", wrapper})
371: 
372: 	result, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
373: 	if err != nil {
374: 		return fmt.Errorf(
375: 			"failed to install config save wrapper on node %q: %w",
376: 			n.Cfg.ShortName,
377: 			err,
378: 		)
379: 	}
380: 	if result.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
381: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to install config save wrapper on node %q: %s",
382: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, result.GetStdErrString())
383: 	}
384: 
385: 	return nil
386: }
387: 
388: // addSSHKeys adds the public keys containerlab collected from the host to the
389: // root and admin users' authorized_keys, to enable passwordless ssh. root gets
390: // a shell and admin gets vtysh, and both are reached with the same key.
391: func (n *frr) addSSHKeys(ctx context.Context) error {
392: 	if len(n.sshPubKeys) == 0 {
393: 		return nil
394: 	}
395: 
396: 	keys := strings.Join(clabutils.MarshalSSHPubKeys(n.sshPubKeys), "\n")
397: 
398: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(
399: 		[]string{"bash", "-c", authzKeysScript, "--", keys})
400: 
401: 	execResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
402: 	if err != nil {
403: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to add ssh keys to node %q: %w", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
404: 	}
405: 
406: 	if execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
407: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to add ssh keys to node %q: %s",
408: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())
409: 	}
410: 
411: 	return nil
412: }
413: 
414: // setPassword sets the password of the node's credentials user: admin with
415: // password admin, unless the topology's credentials say otherwise. The image
416: // ships admin without a password, so a well-known one is not baked into it;
417: // this is what makes the kind's default, or a topology's own credentials, work
418: // for a password login.
419: func (n *frr) setPassword(ctx context.Context) error {
420: 	user := n.Cfg.Credentials.Username
421: 	password := n.Cfg.Credentials.Password
422: 
423: 	if user == "" || password == "" {
424: 		return nil
425: 	}
426: 
427: 	// chpasswd reads one user:password pair per line, so a line break in the
428: 	// password would start a second pair, for whichever user it names.
429: 	if strings.ContainsAny(password, "\r\n") {
430: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q: password must not contain a line break", n.Cfg.ShortName)
431: 	}
432: 
433: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(
434: 		[]string{"bash", "-c", passwordScript, "--", user, password})
435: 
436: 	execResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
437: 	if err != nil {
438: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set password on node %q: %w", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
439: 	}
440: 
441: 	if execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
442: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set password on node %q: %s",
443: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())
444: 	}
445: 
446: 	return nil
447: }
448: 
449: // normalizeConfigFile hands the node's frr.conf back to the user who ran
450: // containerlab. FRR writes that file as frr:frr mode 0600 whenever the node is
451: // asked to "write memory", and it is bind mounted, so the ownership lands on
452: // the lab directory copy and leaves the user unable to read their own saved
453: // configuration without root. Every other file containerlab puts in a lab
454: // directory goes through CreateFile, which does the same thing.
455: func normalizeConfigFile(path string) error {
456: 	// Nothing to fix up when the config was suppressed and never written.
457: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path) {
458: 		return nil
459: 	}
460: 
461: 	err := clabutils.SetUIDAndGID(path)
462: 	if err != nil {
463: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set ownership of %s: %w", path, err)
464: 	}
465: 
466: 	// Both os.WriteFile and os.Create apply a mode only when they create the
467: 	// file, so a 0600 that FRR left behind survives the write and has to be
468: 	// reset explicitly.
469: 	err = os.Chmod(path, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)
470: 	if err != nil {
471: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set permissions of %s: %w", path, err)
472: 	}
473: 
474: 	return nil
475: }
476: 
477: func (n *frr) SaveConfig(ctx context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
478: 	cmd, _ := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(saveCmd)
479: 
480: 	execResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
481: 	if err != nil {
482: 		return nil, err
483: 	}
484: 
485: 	if execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
486: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to save config on node %q: %s",
487: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())
488: 	}
489: 
490: 	confPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)
491: 
492: 	err = os.WriteFile(confPath, execResult.GetStdOutByteSlice(),
493: 		clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)
494: 	if err != nil {
495: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to write config by %s path from %s container: %w",
496: 			confPath, n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
497: 	}
498: 
499: 	err = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)
500: 	if err != nil {
501: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("node=%s: %w", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
502: 	}
503: 
504: 	log.Infof("saved FRR configuration from %s node to %s\n", n.Cfg.ShortName, confPath)
505: 
506: 	// Reporting the path is what makes "containerlab save --copy" pick the file
507: 	// up. A nil result is how a kind says it cannot save at all, and copying is
508: 	// skipped for it without an error, so returning one here loses the config
509: 	// quietly.
510: 	return &clabnodes.SaveConfigResult{
511: 		ConfigPath: confPath,
512: 	}, nil
513: }

        "text": "// Copyright 2020 Nokia\n// Licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License.\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-3-Clause\n\npackage frr\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t_ \"embed\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"path/filepath\"\n\t\"regexp\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"github.com/charmbracelet/log\"\n\t\"golang.org/x/crypto/ssh\"\n\n\tclabconstants \"github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants\"\n\tclabexec \"github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/exec\"\n\tclabnodes \"github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes\"\n\tclabtypes \"github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types\"\n\tclabutils \"github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils\"\n)\n\nvar kindNames = []string{\"frr\", \"frrouting\"}\n\nconst (\n\tgenerateable     = true\n\tgenerateIfFormat = \"eth%d\"\n\n\t// cfgDir holds the three files bind mounted over the container's /etc/frr.\n\tcfgDir = \"config\"\n\n\tfrrConfFile   = \"frr.conf\"\n\tdaemonsFile   = \"daemons\"\n\tvtyshConfFile = \"vtysh.conf\"\n\n\tetcFRR = \"/etc/frr\"\n\n\t// authzKeysTargets are the \"user:home\" pairs whose authorized_keys file is\n\t// populated: root for a shell and admin for vtysh, both reached with the\n\t// same key.\n\tauthzKeysTargets = \"root:/root admin:/home/admin\"\n\n\t// authzKeysGuard skips a user the image does not have. admin ships only in\n\t// the containerlab flavour, so a plain release image still gets root's keys\n\t// instead of an error.\n\tauthzKeysGuard = `id -u \"$user\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || continue`\n\n\t// authzKeysChown hands the file to the user who logs in with it; sshd\n\t// rejects an authorized_keys that user does not own.\n\tauthzKeysChown = `chown -R \"$user:\" \"$home/.ssh\"`\n\n\t// authzKeysScript writes the public keys, passed to it as $1, to the\n\t// authorized_keys of every user in authzKeysTargets that exists.\n\tauthzKeysScript = `set -e\nfor entry in ` + authzKeysTargets + `; do\n\tuser=${entry%%:*}\n\thome=${entry#*:}\n\n\t` + authzKeysGuard + `\n\n\tmkdir -p \"$home/.ssh\"\n\tchmod 700 \"$home/.ssh\"\n\t# The file is removed rather than truncated: a redirect onto an existing\n\t# file keeps that file's ownership, and sshd rejects an authorized_keys\n\t# that its user does not own.\n\trm -f \"$home/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n\tprintf '%s\\n' \"$1\" > \"$home/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n\tchmod 600 \"$home/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n\t` + authzKeysChown + `\ndone`\n\n\t// passwordGuard skips a user the image does not have. A plain release image\n\t// has no admin, and root there is still reachable with the host's keys.\n\tpasswordGuard = `id -u \"$1\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0`\n\n\t// passwordScript sets the password of the user named by $1 to $2. Both\n\t// travel as arguments rather than in the script text, so no character in\n\t// the password can be read as shell.\n\tpasswordScript = passwordGuard + `\nprintf '%s:%s\\n' \"$1\" \"$2\" | chpasswd`\n\n\t// watchfrr invokes vtysh -w for CLI saves. Repair the bind-mounted file\n\t// before returning, while ordinary vtysh sessions still exec the binary.\n\tconfigSaveWrapper = `#!/bin/sh\ncase \"$*\" in\n    -w|--writeconfig)\n        \"/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab\" \"$@\" || exit $?\n        chown %d:%d \"/etc/frr/frr.conf\" || exit $?\n        chmod %04o \"/etc/frr/frr.conf\"\n        ;;\n    *)\n        exec \"/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab\" \"$@\"\n        ;;\nesac`\n\n\t// no-header keeps the \"Building configuration...\" preamble out of the\n\t// saved file, which is written back as the node's frr.conf.\n\tsaveCmd = `vtysh -c \"show running-config no-header\"`\n)\n\nvar (\n\t// configErrorLine matches the lines in which vtysh names a line of the\n\t// configuration it could not parse, e.g. \"line 5: % Unknown command[21]: ...\".\n\tconfigErrorLine = regexp.MustCompile(`^\\s*line \\d+:`)\n\n\t// defaultCredentials is the admin user the containerlab image ships, whose\n\t// login shell is vtysh. It is what \"ssh <node>\" uses, so a bare ssh to a\n\t// node reaches the routing CLI; \"ssh root@<node>\" still gets a shell. The\n\t// image has no password for admin: PostDeploy sets this one.\n\tdefaultCredentials = clabnodes.NewCredentials(\"admin\", \"admin\")\n\n\t//go:embed frr.cfg\n\tdefaultCfgTemplate string\n\n\t// vtyshCfg turns on integrated config so that frr.conf is the single\n\t// configuration file, matching what FRR ships upstream.\n\t//go:embed vtysh.conf\n\tvtyshCfg string\n)\n\n// KindSpecificConfig is the frr kind-specific config, set as keys on the node definition.\ntype KindSpecificConfig struct {\n\t// Daemons is the list of FRR routing daemons to enable. When empty, all\n\t// daemons known to the kind are enabled. The always-on daemons (zebra,\n\t// staticd, mgmtd, watchfrr) need not be listed.\n\tDaemons []string `yaml:\"daemons,omitempty\" json:\"daemons,omitempty\"`\n}\n\nvar kindSpecificConfig clabnodes.KindSpecificConfigSpec[KindSpecificConfig]\n\nfunc (n *frr) kindSpecificCfg() *KindSpecificConfig { return kindSpecificConfig.Of(n.Cfg) }\n\n// Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.\nfunc Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {\n\tgenerateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)\n\n\t// FRR has no scrapli or napalm platform, so no PlatformAttrs are set.\n\tnrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(\n\t\tdefaultCredentials, generateNodeAttributes, nil,\n\t).WithKindSpecificConfig(kindSpecificConfig)\n\n\tr.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {\n\t\treturn new(frr)\n\t}, nrea)\n}\n\ntype frr struct {\n\tclabnodes.DefaultNode\n\n\tsshPubKeys []ssh.PublicKey\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {\n\t// Init DefaultNode\n\tn.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)\n\n\tn.Cfg = cfg\n\tfor _, o := range opts {\n\t\to(n)\n\t}\n\n\t// The whole directory is mounted rather than the three files individually.\n\t// FRR saves a configuration by renaming frr.conf to frr.conf.sav and\n\t// writing a new one, and a single-file bind mount cannot be renamed, so\n\t// per-file mounts make every \"write memory\" report\n\t//\n\t//\tError renaming /etc/frr/frr.conf to /etc/frr/frr.conf.sav: Resource busy\n\t//\n\t// and lose the backup. Mounting the directory costs nothing: the image\n\t// ships only daemons in /etc/frr, and containerlab writes that file too.\n\tn.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds,\n\t\tfmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir), \":\", etcFRR),\n\t)\n\n\t// FRR programs routes into the kernel, which only forwards if asked to.\n\tn.Cfg.Sysctls[\"net.ipv4.ip_forward\"] = \"1\"\n\tn.Cfg.Sysctls[\"net.ipv6.conf.all.forwarding\"] = \"1\"\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) PreDeploy(_ context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {\n\tclabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)\n\n\t_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Recorded here and written to the container in PostDeploy, once it runs.\n\tn.sshPubKeys = params.SSHPubKeys\n\n\treturn n.createFRRFiles()\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) createFRRFiles() error {\n\tnodeCfg := n.Config()\n\n\tdir := filepath.Join(nodeCfg.LabDir, cfgDir)\n\tclabutils.CreateDirectory(dir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)\n\n\t// frr.conf comes from the user's startup-config when given, and from the\n\t// bundled template otherwise. Routing it through GenerateConfig is what\n\t// makes enforce-startup-config, suppress-startup-config and templating\n\t// behave the same way here as for every other kind.\n\tcfgTemplate := defaultCfgTemplate\n\n\tif nodeCfg.StartupConfig != \"\" {\n\t\tc, err := os.ReadFile(nodeCfg.StartupConfig)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tcfgTemplate = string(c)\n\t}\n\n\tconfPath := filepath.Join(dir, frrConfFile)\n\n\terr := n.GenerateConfig(confPath, cfgTemplate)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s, failed to generate config: %w\", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// GenerateConfig keeps an existing frr.conf, which may be one a previous\n\t// \"write memory\" left owned by frr and unreadable, so repair it here too\n\t// rather than only on save.\n\terr = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s: %w\", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\trendered, err := renderDaemons(n.kindSpecificCfg().Daemons)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s: %w\", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\terr = clabutils.CreateFile(filepath.Join(dir, daemonsFile), rendered)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s, failed to write daemons file: %w\", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\terr = clabutils.CreateFile(filepath.Join(dir, vtyshConfFile), vtyshCfg)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s, failed to write vtysh.conf: %w\", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// PostDeploy installs login credentials and keeps CLI-saved configs readable.\nfunc (n *frr) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {\n\tlog.Debugf(\"Running postdeploy actions for frr %q node\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\n\terr := n.addSSHKeys(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = n.setPassword(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = n.installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Last, so that a node with a broken configuration can still be logged\n\t// into to look at it.\n\treturn n.checkConfig(ctx)\n}\n\n// checkConfig fails the deploy when the node's frr.conf has lines FRR cannot\n// parse. FRR skips such a line, applies the rest of the file and keeps\n// running, and says so only in its log, so a typo would otherwise deploy\n// successfully with part of the configuration missing. vtysh's dry-run mode\n// parses the file without applying it, so it needs no running daemons.\n//\n// It catches what vtysh itself rejects: unknown commands, bad values and\n// incomplete commands. A line that parses but that a daemon refuses, or\n// configuration for a daemon that is not enabled, still goes unnoticed.\nfunc (n *frr) checkConfig(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tconfPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)\n\n\t// suppress-startup-config leaves no frr.conf to check.\n\tif !clabutils.FileExists(confPath) {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tcmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(\n\t\t[]string{\"vtysh\", \"--dryrun\", \"--inputfile\", filepath.Join(etcFRR, frrConfFile)})\n\n\tresult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to check the FRR configuration: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif result.GetReturnCode() == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\"%s has errors that FRR skips at startup, leaving those lines unconfigured:\\n%s\\n%s\",\n\t\tconfPath, configCheckErrors(result), n.configFixHint())\n}\n\n// configCheckErrors returns the lines of a failed dry run that name a line of\n// frr.conf, or all of its output if none does.\nfunc configCheckErrors(result *clabexec.ExecResult) string {\n\tout := strings.TrimSpace(result.GetStdOutString() + \"\\n\" + result.GetStdErrString())\n\n\tvar errs []string\n\n\tfor l := range strings.SplitSeq(out, \"\\n\") {\n\t\tif configErrorLine.MatchString(l) {\n\t\t\terrs = append(errs, \"  \"+strings.TrimSpace(l))\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif len(errs) == 0 {\n\t\treturn \"  \" + strings.ReplaceAll(out, \"\\n\", \"\\n  \")\n\t}\n\n\treturn strings.Join(errs, \"\\n\")\n}\n\n// configFixHint says where to fix the configuration. Where it came from a\n// startup-config, the lab directory holds a copy that later deploys keep, so\n// fixing the startup-config alone would not reach the node.\nfunc (n *frr) configFixHint() string {\n\tswitch {\n\tcase n.Cfg.StartupConfig == \"\":\n\t\treturn \"Fix it, then destroy the lab without --cleanup and deploy again, \" +\n\t\t\t\"which keeps the file.\"\n\tcase n.Cfg.EnforceStartupConfig:\n\t\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"It is copied from %s on every deploy: fix that file and deploy again.\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.StartupConfig)\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"It is the lab's copy of %s, which later deploys keep: \"+\n\t\t\t\"fix that file and deploy again with --reconfigure.\", n.Cfg.StartupConfig)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tuid, gid, err := clabutils.GetRealUserIDs()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// FRR can execute vtysh during PostDeploy. A same-directory hard link keeps\n\t// the original available and preserves its metadata, including symlink identity.\n\t// Publish the wrapper atomically only after the backup exists.\n\t// Preserve the original backup across sequential PostDeploy calls.\n\tscript := `set -e\nwrapper_tmp=''\ntrap 'rm -f -- \"$wrapper_tmp\"' EXIT\nif [ ! -f \"/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab\" ]; then\n    ln -T -- \"/usr/bin/vtysh\" \"/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab\"\nfi\nwrapper_tmp=$(mktemp \"/usr/bin/vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX\")\nprintf '%s\\n' \"$1\" > \"$wrapper_tmp\"\nchmod 755 \"$wrapper_tmp\"\nmv -f -- \"$wrapper_tmp\" \"/usr/bin/vtysh\"`\n\n\twrapper := fmt.Sprintf(configSaveWrapper, uid, gid, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)\n\tcmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice([]string{\"bash\", \"-c\", script, \"--\", wrapper})\n\n\tresult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"failed to install config save wrapper on node %q: %w\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\terr,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\tif result.GetReturnCode() != 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to install config save wrapper on node %q: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, result.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// addSSHKeys adds the public keys containerlab collected from the host to the\n// root and admin users' authorized_keys, to enable passwordless ssh. root gets\n// a shell and admin gets vtysh, and both are reached with the same key.\nfunc (n *frr) addSSHKeys(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tif len(n.sshPubKeys) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tkeys := strings.Join(clabutils.MarshalSSHPubKeys(n.sshPubKeys), \"\\n\")\n\n\tcmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(\n\t\t[]string{\"bash\", \"-c\", authzKeysScript, \"--\", keys})\n\n\texecResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to add ssh keys to node %q: %w\", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tif execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to add ssh keys to node %q: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// setPassword sets the password of the node's credentials user: admin with\n// password admin, unless the topology's credentials say otherwise. The image\n// ships admin without a password, so a well-known one is not baked into it;\n// this is what makes the kind's default, or a topology's own credentials, work\n// for a password login.\nfunc (n *frr) setPassword(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tuser := n.Cfg.Credentials.Username\n\tpassword := n.Cfg.Credentials.Password\n\n\tif user == \"\" || password == \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// chpasswd reads one user:password pair per line, so a line break in the\n\t// password would start a second pair, for whichever user it names.\n\tif strings.ContainsAny(password, \"\\r\\n\") {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q: password must not contain a line break\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\t}\n\n\tcmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(\n\t\t[]string{\"bash\", \"-c\", passwordScript, \"--\", user, password})\n\n\texecResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set password on node %q: %w\", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tif execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set password on node %q: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// normalizeConfigFile hands the node's frr.conf back to the user who ran\n// containerlab. FRR writes that file as frr:frr mode 0600 whenever the node is\n// asked to \"write memory\", and it is bind mounted, so the ownership lands on\n// the lab directory copy and leaves the user unable to read their own saved\n// configuration without root. Every other file containerlab puts in a lab\n// directory goes through CreateFile, which does the same thing.\nfunc normalizeConfigFile(path string) error {\n\t// Nothing to fix up when the config was suppressed and never written.\n\tif !clabutils.FileExists(path) {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\terr := clabutils.SetUIDAndGID(path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set ownership of %s: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Both os.WriteFile and os.Create apply a mode only when they create the\n\t// file, so a 0600 that FRR left behind survives the write and has to be\n\t// reset explicitly.\n\terr = os.Chmod(path, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set permissions of %s: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) SaveConfig(ctx context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {\n\tcmd, _ := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(saveCmd)\n\n\texecResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to save config on node %q: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\tconfPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)\n\n\terr = os.WriteFile(confPath, execResult.GetStdOutByteSlice(),\n\t\tclabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to write config by %s path from %s container: %w\",\n\t\t\tconfPath, n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\terr = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s: %w\", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Infof(\"saved FRR configuration from %s node to %s\\n\", n.Cfg.ShortName, confPath)\n\n\t// Reporting the path is what makes \"containerlab save --copy\" pick the file\n\t// up. <path>/source/head/docs/manual/kinds/frr.md</path>

<content>
1: ---
2: search:
3:   boost: 4
4: kind_code_name: frr
5: kind_display_name: FRRouting
6: ---
7: # -{{ kind_display_name }}-
8: 
9: [-{{ kind_display_name }}-](https://frrouting.org) is an open source internet routing protocol suite for Linux. It is identified with the `-{{ kind_code_name }}-` kind in the [topology file](../topo-def-file.md). The `frrouting` kind name is accepted as an alias.
10: 
11: ## FRR via the `linux` kind
12: 
13: Earlier containerlab labs ran FRR containers with the [`linux`](linux.md) kind, supplying `frr.conf` and `daemons` through explicit bind mounts. The native `frr` kind builds on that work and makes FRR a first-class citizen in containerlab: it manages configuration files, daemon selection, forwarding, SSH public keys, and saving the running configuration.
14: 
15: To adapt an existing lab, change `kind: linux` to `kind: frr`, replace the bind mount for `/etc/frr/frr.conf` with `startup-config`, and replace the `/etc/frr/daemons` bind mount with the `daemons` [kind-specific config key](../nodes.md#kind-specific-config). Remove any bind for `/etc/frr/vtysh.conf`, since the kind generates it. Use the containerlab image below if you need SSH access. Existing labs can continue using the `linux` kind.
16: 
17: ## Getting -{{ kind_display_name }}- image
18: 
19: FRR publishes a containerlab image alongside its plain release image, tagged `containerlab-<version>` in the same [`quay.io/frrouting/frr`](https://quay.io/repository/frrouting/frr) repository. This image was introduced with **FRR 10.7.1** and is published for 10.7.1 and later releases. **Releases older than 10.7.1 will not receive containerlab-native images.**
20: 
21: ```bash
22: docker pull quay.io/frrouting/frr:containerlab-10.7.1
23: ```
24: 
25: The containerlab image uses the corresponding FRR release image as its base and adds:
26: 
27: - An OpenSSH server, started alongside `watchfrr`, with SSH host keys generated on first start so each container has its own keys.
28: - An `admin` user whose login shell is `vtysh`, so SSH opens the routing CLI. Its `frr` and `frrvty` group membership lets it configure the router and save configurations. The existing `root` user retains a Linux shell.
29: - An FRR-specific `/etc/motd` describing the two SSH entry points and how to view FRR logs.
30: - A startup script that removes the management network's IPv4 and IPv6 default routes to keep them out of the lab's routing protocols. The connected management routes remain available.
31: 
32: FRR itself and the other base image components are unchanged. The image's build files and README are maintained upstream in [`docker/containerlab`](https://github.com/FRRouting/frr/tree/master/docker/containerlab).
33: 
34: The plain `<version>` tags ship no SSH server. Use `docker exec` to access those containers, as shown in the `vtysh` and `bash` tabs below.
35: 
36: ## Managing -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes
37: 
38: /// tab | SSH
39: The public keys detected on your host are added to both the `admin` and the `root` user, so no password is needed either way.
40: 
41: `admin` is the default user for these nodes, and its login shell is `vtysh`, so a bare `ssh` reaches the routing CLI:
42: 
43: ```bash
44: ssh <node-name>
45: ```
46: 
47: `root` gets a shell:
48: 
49: ```bash
50: ssh root@<node-name>
51: ```
52: 
53: `admin` can also log in with a password, which is `admin` unless the node's [`credentials`](../nodes.md#credentials) set another. The image ships no password for it; containerlab sets this one when it deploys the node, so changing `credentials` changes the password the node accepts. `root` has no password and accepts keys only.
54: 
55: These SSH commands require the containerlab image described above. For plain release images, use the `vtysh` or `bash` tabs below to access them with `docker exec`.
56: ///
57: /// tab | vtysh
58: FRR's integrated shell is available in the container:
59: 
60: ```bash
61: docker exec -it <node-name> vtysh
62: ```
63: 
64: ///
65: /// tab | bash
66: ```bash
67: docker exec -it <node-name> bash
68: ```
69: 
70: ///
71: 
72: ## Interfaces naming
73: 
74: -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes use the `eth` prefix for their data interfaces, so `eth1`, `eth2` and so on. `eth0` is reserved for the management interface.
75: 
76: ## Node configuration
77: 
78: -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes are configured through three files, which containerlab writes into the node's lab directory under `config/`. That directory is bind mounted over the container's `/etc/frr`:
79: 
80: | File | Contents |
81: | --- | --- |
82: | `frr.conf` | the node's running configuration |
83: | `daemons` | which routing daemons to start |
84: | `vtysh.conf` | `service integrated-vtysh-config`, so `frr.conf` is the only config file |
85: 
86: The official image ships none of `frr.conf` and `vtysh.conf`, and `vtysh` refuses to start without them, which is why all three are always written.
87: 
88: Saving a configuration also leaves a `frr.conf.sav` next to them, which is the previous configuration: FRR keeps one backup by renaming the old file before writing the new one.
89: 
90: Files saved with `write memory` or `copy running-config startup-config` belong to the user who deployed the lab and remain readable on the host with `0644` permissions.
91: 
92: ### Startup configuration
93: 
94: Point `startup-config` at an FRR configuration file to have it used as the node's `frr.conf`:
95: 
96: ```yaml
97: topology:
98:   nodes:
99:     router1:
100:       kind: -{{ kind_code_name }}-
101:       image: quay.io/frrouting/frr:containerlab-10.7.1
102:       startup-config: router1/frr.conf
103: ```
104: 
105: Without a `startup-config` the node comes up with a minimal configuration that only sets `frr defaults traditional` and logging.
106: 
107: The hostname is not set in the generated config on purpose. FRR picks up the container's hostname, which containerlab already sets to the node name.
108: 
109: ### Configuration check
110: 
111: FRR skips any line of `frr.conf` it cannot parse and starts with the rest of the file, so a typo would leave a node running with part of its configuration missing and the mistake visible only in its log. To catch this, the deploy checks each node's `frr.conf` with `vtysh --dryrun` and fails if the file does not parse, quoting each line FRR would skip:
112: 
113: ```
114: deployment failed for one or more nodes: node "router1" post-deploy:
115: clab-frr01/router1/config/frr.conf has errors that FRR skips at startup, leaving those lines unconfigured:
116:   line 5: % Unknown command[21]:  ip adress 10.0.0.1/32
117: It is the lab's copy of router1/frr.conf, which later deploys keep: fix that file and deploy again with --reconfigure.
118: ```
119: 
120: The node that failed the check is left running, with its ssh keys and password in place, so it can be inspected.
121: 
122: The file checked is the lab directory's copy, which is the one the node uses. Created from `startup-config` on the first deploy, it is kept by later deploys unless [`enforce-startup-config`](../nodes.md#enforce-startup-config) is set, which is why a fixed `startup-config` needs `--reconfigure` to reach the node.
123: 
124: The check covers what does not parse: unknown commands, invalid values and incomplete commands. A line that parses but that a daemon then rejects, such as a BGP neighbor assigned to a peer-group that does not exist, still deploys, as does configuration for a daemon that is not enabled in [`daemons`](#daemons). Look for `processing failure` in `docker logs <node>` to find the first kind.
125: 
126: ### Daemons
127: 
128: By default all daemons supported by this kind are enabled. To run only the daemons a lab actually needs, list them under the `daemons` [kind-specific config key](../nodes.md#kind-specific-config):
129: 
130: ```yaml
131: topology:
132:   nodes:
133:     router1:
134:       kind: -{{ kind_code_name }}-
135:       image: quay.io/frrouting/frr:containerlab-10.7.1
136:       daemons:
137:         - ospfd
138:         - bfdd
139: ```
140: 
141: Naming any daemon switches off all the ones you did not name. `zebra`, `staticd`, `mgmtd` and `watchfrr` are always started by FRR and may be listed or left out; either way they run.
142: 
143: The list accepts `bgpd`, `ospfd`, `ospf6d`, `ripd`, `ripngd`, `isisd`, `pimd`, `pim6d`, `ldpd`, `nhrpd`, `eigrpd`, `babeld`, `sharpd`, `pbrd`, `bfdd`, `fabricd`, `vrrpd` and `pathd`. Any other name is an error naming the offending entry.
144: 
145: `daemons` can be set on a group or a kind as well as on a single node, so a whole class of routers can share one daemon list. See the [frr01 lab](../../lab-examples/frr01.md) for that.
146: 
147: /// admonition | Daemons and topology size
148:     type: subtle-note
149: Starting all daemons increases the number of processes and memory usage per node. For larger topologies, list only the daemons the lab needs.
150: ///
151: 
152: ### Saving configuration
153: 
154: `containerlab save` writes each node's running configuration back to `config/frr.conf` in its lab directory:
155: 
156: ```bash
157: containerlab save -t <topology-file>
158: ```
159: 
160: The saved file is reused on subsequent deployments while the lab directory exists. To replace it with `startup-config`, set [`enforce-startup-config`](../nodes.md#enforce-startup-config). Destroying the lab with `--cleanup` removes the saved configuration along with the lab directory; copy configurations you want to keep elsewhere first.
161: 
162: ## Host requirements
163: 
164: -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes enable IPv4 and IPv6 forwarding in the container's network namespace, so no host-level sysctl changes are needed.
165: 
166: ## Lab examples
167: 
168: - [FRR OSPF lab](../../lab-examples/frr01.md)

Reasoning
Now check the base version of frr.go and frr_test.go for comparison, and also read the head frr_test.go around the new tests. Also check `RunExec` behavior — how return code is handled. A key question: does `RunExec` return an error when the command exits nonzero? Let me check the exec package. Also check `GetReturnCode` semantics.

Also important: `vtysh --dryrun` — does it exist? In FRR, `vtysh --dryrun` isn't a real flag... Actually `vtysh` has `-C` (dryrun) option: "--dryrun" is `-C`? Let me check: vtysh options: `-b, --markfile`, `-c, --command`, `-d --vty_socket`, `-E --echo`, `-f, --inputfile`, `-m --markfile`... Actually FRR vtysh supports `--dryrun` since... Let me recall: `vtysh -C` = `--dryrun`. Yes, in FRR's vtysh, `-C, --dryrun  Execute in dryrun mode`. And `--inputfile` is `-f`. Long options: `--dryrun` and `--inputfile` both exist. OK.

But does dryrun return nonzero on parse errors? In vtysh dryrun mode, it processes the file; errors printed. Does it set exit code? Historically vtysh returns 0 even on config errors in some paths; but with `--dryrun`, vtysh's `vtysh_config_from_file` sets error and returns exit status 1? In recent FRR, dryrun returns nonzero if errors. That's runtime behavior I can't verify from source; note as coverage note.

Another point: the check runs `vtysh --dryrun --inputfile /etc/frr/frr.conf`. But wait — the wrapper is installed at /usr/bin/vtysh before checkConfig runs (installConfigSaveWrapper runs before). The wrapper for non `-w` args does `exec /usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab "$@"`. So `vtysh --dryrun ...` goes through the wrapper, fine.

But there's a subtlety: dryrun mode in vtysh — when the inputfile contains config, the dryrun parses it. But does vtysh --dryrun actually fail on unknown commands? In FRR, during dryrun, unknown commands are printed as "% Unknown command..." and vtysh exits with... Let me think about the exit code. In vtysh main, dryrun mode: `if (dryrun) { vtysh_config_from_file(...) ... return 0?` Hmm. Actually there was a change: FRR commit adding exit status for dryrun. In FRR 10.x, `vtysh --dryrun` returns 1 on config errors? I can't verify. The docs claim it fails. Tests mock the runtime so this isn't verified. I'll put in coverage notes as an assumption to verify against a real image; the test with rc=4 etc. suggests the author believes nonzero. Given CI frr-tests.yml exists (a GitHub workflow for frr kind), maybe an integration test covers it. Let me check .github/workflows/frr-tests.yml for a dryrun-based test.

Also potential issue: `checkConfig` runs even when the config is fine — extra exec per node, negligible.

Another point: `configErrorLine` regex `^\s*line \d+:` — vtysh output for errors is like "line 5: % Unknown command[21]: ...". OK.

Potential issue: RunExec error handling — when the command fails to start (e.g., image lacks vtysh, plain release image), RunExec returns err, and checkConfig fails the whole deploy with "failed to check the FRR configuration". Wait — but plain release images: the frr kind docs say the containerlab image is needed? Actually the kind works with plain release images too (docs show `docker exec` for plain images). Hmm, does the frr kind require the containerlab image? The docs: "The plain <version> tags ship no SSH server. Use docker exec..." — suggesting the kind can run plain images. In that case, PostDeploy's addSSHKeys would still run and fail? addSSHKeys with no keys returns nil. setPassword returns nil if no credentials. installConfigSaveWrapper: it tries to hard link /usr/bin/vtysh — a plain image has vtysh, so it works. checkConfig: vtysh exists in plain images too. OK.

But wait: what if `vtysh --dryrun` isn't supported by older FRR versions? The docs say images containerlab-10.7.1+. Plain images could be older FRR versions (e.g., frr 8.x) where `--dryrun` long option exists? vtysh has had `--dryrun`? Checking memory: FRR vtysh long options include `--dryrun` (yes, it's `-C, --dryrun`). That's been there for a long time. OK.

Now, a real correctness concern: does `vtysh --dryrun` exit nonzero on parse errors? Let me recall the FRR source: in vtysh/vtysh_main.c, after `vtysh_read_config(config_default_file)` in dryrun mode... Actually the flow: with `--inputfile`, vtysh reads the file via `vtysh_read_config`. Errors in config parsing produce "% Unknown command" lines via `vtysh_config_parse_line`... The exit code: In `vtysh_read_config`, there's `ret = vtysh_config_from_file(...)`. Hmm.

Actually, I recall FRR added: "vtysh: return non-zero exit status on config error in dryrun mode" — commit in FRR 9.1? There is indeed a known behavior: `vtysh -C -f file` returns 1 if the config has errors. Let me search: FRR PR #13085? I can't verify externally. This is a plausible assumption by the author; tests mock it. I'll note as coverage: the nonzero-exit-on-parse-error behavior of `vtysh --dryrun` is asserted by docs and tests but not verified against a real FRR image in this snapshot (unit tests mock the runtime). Note it.

668: // The dry run parses the file the node actually uses, without applying it.
669: func TestCheckConfigPassesValidConfig(t *testing.T) {
670: 	var ran []string
671: 
672: 	n := newCheckNode(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "router1"}, 0, "", "", &ran)
673: 
674: 	if err := n.checkConfig(context.Background()); err != nil {
675: 		t.Fatalf("checkConfig() = %v, want nil", err)
676: 	}
677: 
678: 	want := []string{"vtysh", "--dryrun", "--inputfile", "/etc/frr/frr.conf"}
679: 	if strings.Join(ran, " ") != strings.Join(want, " ") {
680: 		t.Errorf("ran %q, want %q", ran, want)
681: 	}
682: }
683: 
684: // FRR skips a line it cannot parse and starts without it, so the deploy has to
685: // fail, naming the file and each offending line wherever vtysh printed it.
686: func TestCheckConfigFailsOnParseErrors(t *testing.T) {
687: 	var ran []string
688: 
689: 	stdout := "% Can't open configuration file /etc/frr/vtysh.conf due to 'No such file'.\n" +
690: 		"line 5: % Unknown command[21]:  ip adress 10.0.0.1/32\n"
691: 	stderr := "line 9: % Command incomplete[44]:  network 10.0.0.1/32\n"
692: 
693: 	n := newCheckNode(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "router1"}, 4, stdout, stderr, &ran)
694: 
695: 	err := n.checkConfig(context.Background())
696: 	if err == nil {
697: 		t.Fatal("checkConfig() = nil, want an error for a config that does not parse")
698: 	}
699: 
700: 	msg := err.Error()
701: 	for _, want := range []string{
702: 		filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile),
703: 		"line 5: % Unknown command[21]:  ip adress 10.0.0.1/32",
704: 		"line 9: % Command incomplete[44]:  network 10.0.0.1/32",
705: 	} {
706: 		if !strings.Contains(msg, want) {
707: 			t.Errorf("error %q does not contain %q", msg, want)
708: 		}
709: 	}
710: 
711: 	// Only the lines that point into frr.conf are worth repeating.
712: 	if strings.Contains(msg, "vtysh.conf") {
713: 		t.Errorf("error repeats unrelated vtysh output: %q", msg)
714: 	}
715: }
716: 
717: // Should vtysh fail without naming a line, its output is all there is to go on.
718: func TestCheckConfigReportsUnstructuredFailure(t *testing.T) {
719: 	var ran []string
720: 
721: 	n := newCheckNode(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "router1"},
722: 		1, "", "vtysh: something unexpected\n", &ran)
723: 
724: 	err := n.checkConfig(context.Background())
725: 	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "vtysh: something unexpected") {
726: 		t.Fatalf("checkConfig() = %v, want the raw vtysh output", err)
727: 	}
728: }
729:

731: // check; the mock fails the test on any Exec.

734: 	n.WithRuntime(clabmocksmockruntime.NewMockContainerRuntime(gomock.NewController(t)))

736: 	if err := n.checkConfig(context.Background()); err != nil {
737: 		t.Fatalf("checkConfig() = %v, want nil", err)
738: 	}
739: }
740: 
741: // The lab directory keeps its frr.conf across deploys unless the startup-config
742: // is enforced, so where to fix the configuration depends on where it came from.
743: func TestConfigFixHint(t *testing.T) {
744: 	for _, tt := range []struct {
745: 		name    string
746: 		cfg     clabtypes.NodeConfig
747: 		want    string
748: 		notWant string
749: 	}{
750: 		{
751: 			name:    "no startup-config",
752: 			cfg:     clabtypes.NodeConfig{},
753: 			want:    "without --cleanup",
754: 			notWant: "--reconfigure",
755: 		},
756: 		{
757: 			name:    "startup-config",
758: 			cfg:     clabtypes.NodeConfig{StartupConfig: "/topo/r1.conf"},
759: 			want:    "fix that file and deploy again with --reconfigure",
760: 			notWant: "--cleanup",
761: 		},
762: 		{
763: 			name:    "enforced startup-config",
764: 			cfg:     clabtypes.NodeConfig{StartupConfig: "/topo/r1.conf", EnforceStartupConfig: true},
765: 			want:    "copied from /topo/r1.conf on every deploy",
766: 			notWant: "--reconfigure",
767: 		},
768: 	} {
769: 		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
770: 			cfg := tt.cfg
771: 			cfg.ShortName = "router1"
772: 
773: 			got := newTestNode(t, &cfg).configFixHint()
774: 			if !strings.Contains(got, tt.want) {
775: 				t.Errorf("hint %q does not contain %q", got, tt.want)
776: 			}
777: 
778: 			if strings.Contains(got, tt.notWant) {
779: 				t.Errorf("hint %q should not mention %q", got, tt.notWant)
780: 			}
781: 		})
782: 	}
783: }

785: // PostDeploy runs the check after everything else, so a failed deploy still
786: // leaves a node that can be logged into, and returns its error so the deploy
787: // fails.
788: func TestPostDeployFailsOnBadConfig(t *testing.T) {
789: 	n := newTestNode(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "router1"})
790: 
791: 	dir := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir)
792: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
793: 		t.Fatal(err)
794: 	}
795: 
796: 	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, frrConfFile), []byte("hostname r1\n"), 0o644); err != nil {
797: 		t.Fatal(err)
798: 	}
799: 
800: 	var ran [][]string
801: 
802: 	rt := clabmocksmockruntime.NewMockContainerRuntime(gomock.NewController(t))
803: 	n.WithRuntime(rt)
804: 	rt.EXPECT().Exec(gomock.Any(), gomock.Any(), gomock.Any()).DoAndReturn(
805: 		func(_ context.Context, _ string, cmd *clabexec.ExecCmd) (*clabexec.ExecResult, error) {
806: 			ran = append(ran, cmd.GetCmd())
807: 
808: 			res := clabexec.NewExecResult(cmd)
809: 			if cmd.GetCmd()[0] == "vtysh" {
810: 				res.SetReturnCode(2)
811: 				res.SetStdOut([]byte("line 1: % Unknown command[21]: hostnme r1\n"))
812: 			}
813: 
814: 			return res, nil
815: 		}).Times(2)
816: 
817: 	err := n.PostDeploy(context.Background(), nil)
818: 	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "hostnme r1") {
819: 		t.Fatalf("PostDeploy() = %v, want the config check's error", err)
820: 	}
821: 
822: 	if len(ran) != 2 || ran[len(ran)-1][0] != "vtysh" {
823: 		t.Errorf("exec calls %q: the config check must run last", ran)
824: 	}

1: package exec

3: import (
4: 	"encoding/json"
5: 	"errors"

7: 	"strings"
8: 	"sync"
9:
